体增速。
投资再度走弱。10 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 8.1%，降幅较前三季
度扩大 0.9 个百分点。按照累计投资额和累计增速粗略反推，10 月单月固
定资产投资同比降幅约为 16.9%，9 月的单月企稳并未延续。扣除房地产
后的固定资产投资累计下降约 5.1%，降幅也较前三季度扩大约 0.9 个百
分点；民间投资下降 11.4%，降幅扩大 1.0 个百分点，指向投资弱化不只
是地产问题。
制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域。10 月，制造业累计
投资下降 2.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.8 个百分点。国家统计局目前公
布的 13 个制造业行业中，仅纺织业、铁路船舶航空航天和计算机通信电
子设备制造业投资保持正增长；正增长行业数量由前三季度的 5 个减少至
3 个。具体看，一方面，优势产业仍然具备需求、生产和资本开支相互支
撑的条件。更宽口径的高技术产业投资增长 4.0%，其中信息服务、航空
航天设备、电子及通信设备投资分别增长 20.5%、10.2%和 6.6%。另一

方面，部分上游原料料行业和下游制造业投资维持负增。农副食品、食
品、化工、医药、金属制品、专用设备和汽车制造业投资分别下降
4.1%、10.7%、8.4%、9.2%、9.9%、9.0%和 7.9%。
地产投资降幅扩大，现房销售制度下销售回款向开发投资传导节奏出现变
化。10 月，地产投资累计同比下降 21.6%，降幅较前三季度扩大 1.0 个
百分点；按累计金额反推，10 月单月投资下降约 33.2%，较 9 月约
26.3%的降幅进一步扩大。结构上看，一方面， 地产销售存在局部企
稳。销售端看，10 月，单月商品房销售面积和销售额分别下降约 19.1%
和 18.6%，降幅较 9 月分别收窄约 1.6 个和 1.5 个百分点；二手房交易网
签面积累计增长 11.4%。价格端看，10 月，一、二、三线城市新房价格
同比降幅分别收窄 0.4 个、0.4 个和 0.3 个百分点，二手房价格同比降幅
也均有收窄；环比看，一线城市新房价格环比由上涨转为持平，二线城市
由持平转为下降，或指向价格整体修复动能不足。另一方面，现房销售制

度改革背景下销售回款对投资的前置融资功能减弱。资金端看，1—10 月
房地产开发企业到位资金下降 21.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.3 个百分
点。其中，国内贷款和自筹资金分别下降 33.4%和 21.9%，定金及预收款
下降 15.5%，个人按揭贷款下降 22.1%。到位资金降幅明显大于商品房销
售额 14.3%的累计降幅，一定程度上表明销售收入未同步转化为开发企业
可以用于投资的有效资金。供给端看，1—10 月房屋施工面积下降
13.0%，竣工面积下降 26.6%，降幅较前三季度分别扩大 0.2 个和 1.5 个
百分点；新开工面积下降 24.4%，降幅小幅收窄，仍处较低水平。
基建延续回落，资金尚未转化为实物工作量。10 月，基建投资累计同比
下降 5.1%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.9 个百分点。其中，电热燃水投资下
降 7.7%，交通运输仓储邮政投资增长 0.7%，水利环境和公共设施管理投
资下降 14.4%，均较前三季度有所放缓。我们分析，政策资金到项目建设
存在传导时滞是一个重要原因。截至 10 月 28 日，8000 亿元新型政策性

### Page 3

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 3 
内部尚存在结构性支撑。1—10 月信息传输业、航空运输业和水上运输业
投资分别增长 32.0%、12.5%和 12.5%，数字基础设施及部分交通网络仍
保持较快增长。 
商品消费再度转弱，服务消费提供相对支撑。9 月，社零同比增长
0.4%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。其中，商品和餐饮
消费均有所放缓，分别增长 0.3%和 0.7%，较 9 月分别下降 0.7 个和 1.4
个百分点。结构上看，一方面，商品消费走弱范围扩大。10 月汽车类零
售额下降 21.4%，降幅与 9 月基本持平；剔除汽车后，消费品零售额增长
2.5%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点。9 月相对较强的通讯器材类零售增速由
31.1%降至 6.5%，服装和日用品分别下降 2.0%和 0.4%，金银珠宝下降
28.7%，建筑装潢材料下降 11.2%。另一方面，服务消费仍具有相对韧
性。1—10 月服务零售额增长 4.9%，高于商品零售额的 0.9%；网上服务
零售额增长 5.0%，快于网上商品零售额的 2.9%。通讯信息服务、旅游咨
询租赁和文体休闲服务增长较快，指向服务消费较商品消费更具韧性。 
往后看，未来经济的增量支撑仍主要来自两条线索。一是投资端的政策重
点正由资金投放转向项目实施和实物工作量形成。截至 10 月 28 日，
8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具已经完成投放；11 月 6 日国常会进一步提
出加快重大项目和“六张网”建设实施进度，用好用足各项政策性资金，
及时足额拨付涉及基层和企业的各类款项，并支持企业扩大设备更新和无
形资产投资，指向当前政策正在着力打通资金拨付和项目施工之间的堵
点。二是消费政策由商品补贴向服务供给和消费基础设施延伸。国常会提
出加强消费线上线下、城乡联动，畅通投资与消费良性循环，叠加此前消
费基础设施建设政策对文旅、体育、养老托育、家政和健康等领域的部
署，我们预计服务消费仍会快于商品消费。

风险提示：实物工作量形成不及预期、AI 产业链超预期降温、外需边际走
弱。

1. 生产：工业生产增速回落，外需支撑优势制造业高增 
工业生产回落。10 月，规上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，
环比增长 0.2%，低于季节性。分门类看，采矿业、制造业和电热燃水业增加值分别增长
0.7%、5.4%和 4.4%，较 9 月分别下降 0.6 个、0.6 个和 1.2 个百分点，工业生产呈现较
为广泛的降温。我们分析工业生产放缓主要有两方面拖累，一是地产、基建及部分传统
制造业需求继续走弱，生产改善不具备向更广泛行业扩散的条件。10 月，非金制品、黑
金冶炼和有色冶炼增加值分别下降 2.3%、1.7%和 4.4%；钢材、水泥、平板玻璃和十种
有色金属产量分别下降 5.1%、1.3%、16.0%和 1.2%。化工和橡胶塑料制品增加值也仅
增长 1.3%和 1.2%。汽车制造业增加值增速由 6.7%降至 0.9%，汽车产量下降 7.7%。
二是国际能源价格再次上涨强化中下游制造业成本约束，可能对部分行业扩产形成压制。
10 月布伦特原油现货均价升至每桶 124 美元，较 9 月上涨约 10 美元。同期工业生产者
购进价格同比上涨 7.9%，高于出厂价格 5.7%的涨幅，其中燃料动力类和化工原料类购
进价格分别上涨 18.6%和 16.8%；制造业 PMI 原材料购进价格指数为 56.8%，仍处于
较高扩张区间。从生产数据看，10 月原油加工量下降 8.1%，化工、橡胶塑料等成本敏
感行业增加值仅低位增长。我们分析，油价上涨未必是工业生产回落的直接原因，但叠
加内需偏弱、成本传导不畅，可能会放大成本约束，挤压部分中下游企业的扩产空间。  
工业生产主要增量依旧集中于外需强劲背景下的优势制造领域。10 月，规模以上工
业企业出口交货值增长 18.6%，较 9 月提高 1.6 个百分点；装备制造业出口交货值增长
20.6%，其中汽车、电气机械和电子行业分别增长 52.2%、23.7%和 15.3%，三者对全
部工业出口交货值增长的贡献率合计达到 66.7%。与出口结构相对应，高技术制造业和
装备制造业增加值分别增长 17.6%和 10.6%，计算机通信电子设备、专用设备、铁路船
舶航空航天设备制造业分别增长 19.4%、11.5%和 9.2%；集成电路、工业机器人和民用
钢质船舶产量分别增长 21.9%、26.0%和 35.6%。 
服务业小幅放缓，生产性和数字化服务仍是主要支撑。10 月，服务业生产指数同比
增长 4.6%，较 9 月下降 0.2 个百分点；服务业商务活动指数为 50.0%，较 9 月下降 0.2
个百分点。结构上看，生产性和数字化服务增速较高。租赁和商务服务业、信息传输软
件和信息技术服务业、交通运输仓储和邮政业生产指数分别增长 9.3%、9.2%和 5.1%，
均高于服务业整体增速。

2. 投资：延续回落 
投资再度走弱。10 月，固定资产投资累计同比下降 8.1%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.9
个百分点。按照累计投资额和累计增速粗略反推，10 月单月固定资产投资同比降幅约为
16.9%，9 月的单月企稳并未延续。扣除房地产后的固定资产投资累计下降约 5.1%，降
幅也较前三季度扩大约 0.9 个百分点；民间投资下降 11.4%，降幅扩大 1.0 个百分点，
指向投资弱化不只是地产问题。

制造业投资小幅修复，增长仍集中在少数优势领域。10 月，制造业累计投资下降
2.9%，降幅较前三季度扩大 0.8 个百分点。国家统计局目前公布的 13 个制造业行业中，

3. 消费：再度转弱 
商品消费再度转弱，服务消费提供相对支撑。9 月，社零同比增长 0.4%，较 9 月下
降 0.7 个百分点，环比增长 0.02%。其中，商品和餐饮消费均有所放缓，分别增长 0.3%
和 0.7%，较 9 月分别下降 0.7 个和 1.4 个百分点。结构上看，一方面，商品消费走弱范
围扩大。10 月汽车类零售额下降 21.4%，降幅与 9 月基本持平；剔除汽车后，消费品零
售额增长 2.5%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点。9 月相对较强的通讯器材类零售增速由 31.1%
降至 6.5%，服装和日用品分别下降 2.0%和 0.4%，金银珠宝下降 28.7%，建筑装潢材
料下降 11.2%。另一方面，服务消费仍具有相对韧性。1—10 月服务零售额增长 4.9%，
高于商品零售额的 0.9%；网上服务零售额增长 5.0%，快于网上商品零售额的 2.9%。通
讯信息服务、旅游咨询租赁和文体休闲服务增长较快，指向服务消费较商品消费更具韧
性。

4. 展望：实物工作量形成是重点 
往后看，未来经济的增量支撑仍主要来自两条线索。一是投资端的政策重点正由资
金投放转向项目实施和实物工作量形成。截至 10 月 28 日，8000 亿元新型政策性金融
工具已经完成投放；11 月 6 日国常会进一步提出加快重大项目和“六张网”建设实施进
度，用好用足各项政策性资金，及时足额拨付涉及基层和企业的各类款项，并支持企业
扩大设备更新和无形资产投资，指向当前政策正在着力打通资金拨付和项目施工之间的
堵点。二是消费政策由商品补贴向服务供给和消费基础设施延伸。国常会提出加强消费
线上线下、城乡联动，畅通投资与消费良性循环，叠加此前消费基础设施建设政策对文
旅、体育、养老托育、家政和健康等领域的部署，我们预计服务消费仍会快于商品消费。

Document 036: 2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-13_［みずほ経済EXPRESS］160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない_b6d02f4b.pdf

### Page 1

1

みずほ経済 EXPRESS

160 円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではない 
～164 円への円安も視野、介入警戒の磁力が一段安を阻むか～

調査部  主任エコノミスト 東深澤 武史 
080-1069-4919  takeshi.higashifukasawa@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ ドル円の安定はかりそめ 
9月下旬以降、1ドル＝150円台後半での小動きが続いたが、これをもって「相場が安定している」と
捉えるのは危うい。チャート上は、上値目途として意識される200日移動平均線が150円台半ばに位置
し、同水準へ向けた円安方向への動きが繰り返された（図表1）。このところの値動きの狭さは、円安
方向を根強く試す動きが抵抗線付近で抑えられた結果とみる方が実態に近そうだ。 
11月に入ってからは、円高になりうる要因が散見されたにもかかわらず、市場ではあまり材料視さ
れていない。米国中間選挙（11月3日）の投開票後には、米国のデータセンター建設投資の持続性に対
する懸念が強まったことで株価が大きく下落した（図表2）。震源地である米国から日本に資金を退避
する動きが生じれば円高要因になるが、ドル円の値動きは限定的だった。また、中東情勢に対する楽
観的な見方から原油安が進展しており、円需給の観点からは先行きの本邦輸入金額の減少を通じて円
安圧力を緩和する要因になるが、この点も現時点では円高につながっていない。 
足元では、米利上げ観測の再燃を背景に再び円安が進んでおり、円安圧力は一段と強まっている。
本稿執筆時点では、心理的節目の200日移動平均線を超え、159円台後半まで上昇している。 
図表1 ドル円レート 図表2 日米株価指数

### Page 2

2

本稿では、円安圧力の背景にある要因を改めて整理したうえで、それらを考慮したドル円の推計値
を試算する。実勢値との比較を通じて、足元の円安水準が現在の経済環境に照らしてどの程度説明で
きるのか検証し、その結果をもとに先行きのドル円を展望する。 
■ 現時点でのドル円の適正水準は 1 ドル＝163.70 円 
1ドル＝160円の円安を試す動きは、現在の経済環境に裏付けられたものなのだろうか。この点を検
証するために、東深澤（2026）で構築したモデルを用いて、現在の経済環境から説明されるドル円の
水準を確認する（推計式の詳細や構築過程については東深澤（2026）を参照されたい）。このモデル
を用いて足元の経済環境と整合的なドル円を試算すると、推計値は1ドル＝163.70円と実勢値より円安
になる（図表3）。足元の経済環境下では、160円台はやはり「円安すぎ」ではないということだ。 
モデルが円安水準を支持している理由として最も大きいのが、日本の期待インフレ率の上昇だ。2022
年以降の円安を要因分解すると、米金利上昇や実需の円需給環境の悪化に加え、日本の期待インフレ
率の上昇が大きく寄与している（図表4）。物価が上がれば、同じ金額で買える財・サービスが少なく
なり、円の購買力は低下する。先行きも物価上昇が続くとの見方が強まれば（＝期待インフレ率の上
昇）、将来の円の価値が低下することを織り込む過程で、現在の円相場にも円安圧力がかかると考え
られる。 
実際、日本の期待インフレ率上昇と円安との関係は、データからも確認される。図表5をみると、日
米期待インフレ率格差が縮小すると、円安が進むという対応関係がうかがえる。米国の期待インフレ
率はおおむね安定しており1、日米期待インフレ率格差の縮小は、主に日本の期待インフレ率が上昇し
ていることによる。こうした期待インフレ率の高まりは、賃金・物価上昇の定着にともなう構造的な
変化2だ。期待インフレ率が反転して大幅に低下することは短期的に見込みづらいため、1ドル＝160円
程度の円安水準が定着することは自然だと言える。 
加えて、足元では米金利の上昇も円安圧力となっているようだ。11月以降は、市場予想を上回る物
図表3 ドル円の推計値と実勢値 図表4 2022年以降の円安要因

### Page 3

3

価指標や、ウォラー理事など複数のFRB高官発言がタカ派的と受け止められたことを背景に、米金利
は再度上昇に転じつつある。 
■ 今後は、介入警戒の磁力が一段の円安を阻むかが焦点に 
先行き、推計値（1ドル=163.70円）との乖離を解消する形で円安が進む可能性は十分にある。円安進
行のドライバーになりうるのが、投機筋に積み上がった円買いポジションの巻き戻しだ。図表6は、実
勢値と推計値の乖離を、投機筋の円ポジションに重ねたものだ。これをみると、円買いポジションが
拡大する局面では、実勢値が推計値より円高になる場面が多いことがわかる。とりわけ9月中旬にかけ
ては、日銀の追加利上げ観測などを背景に投機筋の円買い超過幅が拡大した。円買いポジション調整
の余地は十分にあり、巻き戻しの動きが顕在化する場合は1ドル＝164円近傍までの円安進行が視野に
入ってくるということだ。 
こうしたシナリオが実現するかどうかは、為替介入への警戒の持続性に左右される。7月末の日米協
調介入も記憶に新しく、今のところ為替市場は介入への警戒感を維持していると考えられる。11月12
日には三村財務官が円安をけん制する発言を行うなど、日本政府が再び介入姿勢を強めている点を踏
まえれば、160円を持続的に上回るような円安進行は回避されるという見方が穏当だ。 
ただし、リスクは円安方向に大きい。特に、協調介入に対する米国側の姿勢が反転するシナリオに
注意しておきたい。7月当時と比べると、米国政府にとって協調介入の必要性が低下している可能性が
あるためだ。米国政府は当時、日本の長期金利上昇と円安が米国債市場に波及し、米金利を押し上げ
る経路を意識していたとみられる。足元では日米ともに長期金利の上昇が一服しており、日本発の市
場の混乱を防ぐ動機が弱まっている可能性は相応にある。今後、米国政府高官の発言などを機に、米
国が協調介入実施に慎重という見方が広がれば、市場の介入警戒感が和らぎかねない。その場合は、
投機筋のポジション調整を伴う形で、160円を上回って円安が進行するリスクに注意が必要だ。 
以上を踏まえ、2026年末にかけては、米利上げ観測が続き、150円台後半～160円近辺での円安水準
が続く展開を予想する。ただし、投機筋の円買いが巻き戻されたり、協調介入への警戒が和らいだり
図表5 ドル円と日米期待インフレ格差 図表6 推計値と実績値の差及び投機筋の動向

すれば、164円近辺までの円安は十分に射程圏内に入る。為替介入が実施されれば1ドル＝150円程度ま
での一時的な円高は想定されるものの、その後は再び円安を試す動きが強まりやすいだろう。

東深澤武史（2026）「160円台が「円安すぎ」ではない理由～「期待インフレ上昇」という新たな構造
変化」、みずほ総合研究所『みずほ経済 EXPRESS』、2026年7月29日 
東深澤武史ほか（2024）「円安が進むドル円の再評価～緩やかな円高予想も、日米金利や円需給を巡る
不確実性は円安リスクに」、みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ『みずほリポート』、2024年4
月23日

1 この間、米国の期待インフレ率は概ね 2%台半ばで横ばい推移となっている。2026 年の中東情勢悪化を受けて急速な原油高が
進展した場面でも、期待インフレ率の急激な上昇は回避されている。背景には、米国の利上げ期待が急速に進んだことにある。
FRB が迅速に利上げを進めることで、先行きのインフレ率は安定するだろうという市場の期待が反映されていると解釈すること
ができる。 
2 実需要因も、円相場の趨勢を考えるうえで重要な要因である。足元の相場変動への寄与は限定的なため本文では詳述していな
いが、実需面の構造変化に関する分析は東深澤ほか（2024）を参照されたい。

Document 103: 2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026年11月 17日

1/3 
2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し 
～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 2027年の春闘賃上げ率を、厚生労働省ベースで 5.15％（2026年：5.18％）、連合ベースで
4.98％（同 5.01％）と予測する。2027年春闘でもほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、厚生労
働省ベースでは４年連続で５％台が実現するだろう。連合は 2027 年春闘の要求目安を前年
よりやや引き上げ、経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。ＵＡゼンセ
ンも前年並みの高い要求水準を示すなど、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
○ 構造的な人手不足が続くなか、人材の確保・定着に向けた賃上げの必要性は高い。企業収益
も総じて堅調であり、高水準の利益が賃上げ余力を支えている。2026年の物価上昇率が前年
から鈍化することは賃上げ率の下押し要因となる。一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初に
は物価上昇率が再び高まる可能性があり、実質賃金の安定的な改善を求める労使双方の問題
意識も強い。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続
する環境は維持されている。 
○ ４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、日本銀行にとっても金融政策の正常化に向けて前
向きな材料となる。特に、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃
金を設定するようになっているかどうかが、２％の物価安定目標の定着を見極めるうえで重
要となる。

2027年春闘でも前年並みの高い賃上げ率を予想 
2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 5.15％と予測する（厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結
状況」ベース）。2024年春闘では5.33％、2025年では 5.52％、2026年でも 5.18％と３年連続で
５％台の高い賃上げが実現したが、2027年もほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、４年連続で５％
台となるだろう。 
春闘においては、厚生労働省よりも公表時期が早い、連合による集計値が注目されることが多
い。この連合ベースの数字では、2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 4.98％と予測する（2026年春闘：
5.01％）。５％台に乗るかどうかは微妙なところだが、こちらも歴史的にみれば高い賃上げとなる

### Page 2

2/3 
だろう。

組合側、経営側ともトーンダウンはみられず  
組合側、経営側とも賃上げに前向きな姿勢を維持している。連合は 10月22日に公表した2027
年春闘基本構想において、定昇込みの賃上げ要求を「６％中心、５％以上」、ベースアップ等の賃
上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、前年の賃上げ「５％以上」、ベア「３％以上」よりも要求
姿勢をやや強めた。経営側も賃上げの継続に前向きだ。経団連の経営労働政策委員会報告原案で
は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられていると報じられた。また、筒井経団連会長
は 11月９日の記者会見で、ベア実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードとする方針を堅持する考え
を示した。また、主要産業別労働組合組織では、ＵＡゼンセンが定昇込み６％、ベア等４％とい
う前年と同等の高い要求方針素案を示している。中東情勢悪化によるコスト上昇という逆風があ
るなかでも、労使ともに賃上げ姿勢がトーンダウンしている様子は窺えない。もっとも、連合の
要求目安は強まったものの、ＵＡゼンセンの要求率は前年と同水準であり、現時点では賃上げ率
のさらなる加速を見込むほどの材料が揃っているわけではないことには注意が必要だ。

物価は前年から鈍化も、人手不足と好調な企業収益が押し上げ要因に 
高い賃上げを支える最大の要因は、構造的な人手不足である。生産年齢人口の減少が続くな
か、企業の人手不足感は依然として強く、人材の確保・定着は重要な経営課題となっている。特
に若年層を中心に採用競争が厳しくなっており、既存人材のつなぎ止めのためにも企業は継続的
な賃上げを行う必要がある。ここ数年の春闘では、こうした人手不足を背景に高い賃上げが実現
しており、企業の賃金設定行動にも変化が生じている。2027年春闘でも人手不足を背景とした賃
上げ圧力は強いと考えられる。 
好調な企業収益も、高い賃上げを支える材料になる。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇は企業収
益の下押し要因となっているものの、価格転嫁の進展やＡＩ関連需要の拡大などを背景に、企業
収益は総じて堅調に推移しており、水準も非常に高い。企業規模や業種によって差はあるもの
の、企業収益が全体として高水準にあり、賃上げ余力は十分に確保されていると見てよい。 
一方、物価については、2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化することが賃上げ率の下押し要因
になるとみられる。春闘交渉では前年の暦年の物価上昇率が参照されることが多いが、2026年の
上昇率（ＣＰＩ総合）は前年比＋２％前後と、2025年の前年比＋3.2％から鈍化する見込みであ
り、物価高への対応という観点からの賃上げ圧力は弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、暦年でみれば物価上昇率は鈍化する一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初には再び
高まり、ＣＰＩは前年比＋３％台に達する可能性がある。この場合、実質賃金が再びマイナスに
転じる恐れもある。そもそも、過去数年にわたって実質賃金が減少してきたことで、家計には賃
上げの恩恵を十分に実感できないという不満が残っている。実質賃金の改善の遅れへの問題意識
は組合側、経営側にも共有されており、実質賃金を安定的に増加させることは 2027年春闘でも重
要な課題となるだろう。春闘交渉の時点で物価上昇率の再加速が意識されれば、春闘賃上げ率の

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
上振れ要因となる可能性もあることには注意したい。

２％「定着」判断の重要な材料に  
以上を踏まえ、2027年春闘では5.15％と、ほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げを予想する（厚生労働省
ベース）。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続する環境
は維持されていると考えられる。 
４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、賃金上昇の持続性を重視する日本銀行にとっても前
向きな材料となるだろう。特に重要なのは、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを
前提として賃金を設定するようになっているかどうかである。2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍
化するなかでも高い賃上げが維持されれば、こうした賃金設定が広がりつつあることを示す一つ
の材料となる。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定目標とおおむね整合的な水準に達し
たとの認識を示したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判断していない。2027年春
闘は、その定着を見極めるうえでも重要な判断材料となるだろう。

公司簡介 
志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，
以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製程等技
術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相
關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備起家，而後
伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、
面板、太陽能等產業，近年陸續在 IC 載板、
高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值
市場發光發熱。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃
分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、傳
統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。

 投資評價與建議 
維持買進，目標價降至 358 元：(1) 3Q26 獲利表現不如預期，主因 PCB 比重拉升
及面對中國在地價格競爭，(2) 能見度高，但隨企業 AI Server 帶動 HDI/HLC PCB 
與 ABF 載板 2H26-2027 年接力放量，短期獲利改善有限。我們預估 2026/ 2027
年 EPS 為 14.01/19.92 元，目標 PE 則由 30x 下修至 18x，反映市場對設備類股評
價的調整，目標價隨之下修到 358 元(18x 2027 EPSF)。

營業利益(百萬) 毛利率

### Page 2

2

志聖以光與熱為核心，發展熱處理平台：志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製
程等技術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備
起家，而後伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、面板、太陽能等產業，近
年陸續在 IC 載板、高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值市場發光發熱。公司
也與均豪、均華、東捷組成 G2C+聯盟，共同實現台灣先進封裝在地化。志聖主要
生產據點位於台灣林口、台中、中國廣州、昆山，並在台南、廣東、泰國、美國鳳
凰城有服務據點。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、
傳統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。主要競爭對手包含 EVG、TOK、SUSS、
Yield Engineering Systems(YES)等。客戶包含台積電、日月光、臻鼎、力成、
Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等電子中上游廠商。 
3Q26 獲利不如預期：志聖 3Q26 營收 29.8 億(+7.0%QoQ，+93.9%YoY)，毛利
率 43.3%，稅後淨利 5.5 億(-13.4%QoQ，+162.1%YoY)，EPS 3.51 元。毛利率
表現不如預期主因出貨較多中國 PCB 用低階設備，使產品組合轉差，即半導體設
備/PCB 設備由 1H26 的 46%/46%變動至 1-3Q26 的 45%/49%，營業費用亦隨
獎酬增加及研發專案數的提升而高於預期。 
能見度高，但我們對獲利趨於審慎：管理層預期 2026 年底在手訂單約 100-110
億，較去年同期約 43 億元倍增，其中半導體佔比約 53%以上(以 OSAT 能見度較
高)、高階 PCB 35-37%。值得注意的是，半導體設備交期已由 4-10 個月拉長至 4-
13 個月；PCB 設備交期則約 3-8 個月，主因 OSAT 在廠房未完工前即下單，以及
上游零組件(機械手臂)交期的拉長所致，志聖持續以 G2C+聯盟和日系供應商溝通
並聯合採購，目前風險可控。展望後市，我們預期在晶圓廠先進封裝產能建置外溢
的環境下，志聖將透過挑單生產，降低傳統 PCB 接單比例以維持獲利率穩健成長。 
載板挖槽嵌入製程，2028 年再添動能：隨 AI 晶片尺寸持續放大，台系載板廠正
發展新的生產方式，以降低整片翹曲同時達到微縮線寬/線距的目的。志聖在此製
程中提供背面貼膜，以及 ABF 絕緣膜的壓合與撕膜設備。由於層數倍增將使壓合
與撕膜次數同步增加，加上載具尺寸放大推升單機價值，我們認為這將是志聖 
2028 年後載板設備的重要成長來源。 
財務預估：研究部預估志聖 2026 年營收 111.1 億元(+82.2%YoY)，毛利率 46.3%，
稅後淨利 22.7 億元(+173.0%YoY)，EPS 14.01 元(原估 16.94 元，下修主因本業
獲利表現不如預期及股本膨脹)。展望 2027 年，公司尚未給予明確展望，我們考量
短期 PCB/HLC 急單湧現壓抑毛利率，故下修獲利預估，預期 2027 年營收 148.4
億元(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 46.9%，稅後淨利 32.2 億元(+42.2%YoY)，EPS 19.92
元(原估 25.15 元，下修主因獲利預估調降及股本膨脹)。

公司拜訪快報

融程電(3416)：國防軍工引領成長 
建議：維持買進

一、融程電為強固型電腦廠商，
近年國防軍工營收大幅提升 
 融程電成立於 1996 年，公司前三大股東為研華、醫揚、廣積。融程電為強固型
平板電腦和手持式裝置專業廠商，產品廣泛應用於航太、船舶、軍警、交通運輸、
車廠維修檢測、醫管、石化、和製程控制等利基型產業；歐洲為融程電最大市場，
賓士、保時捷、Bosch 等皆為融程電客戶，美洲市場方面，智慧倉儲解決方案廠
商 Honeywell、堆高機品牌 CROWN 以及智慧醫療 Medtronic 等亦是融程電客
戶。近年國防軍工營收大幅提升，自 2024 年的高個位數佔比提升至 2025 年的
近兩成水準，2026 年已突破兩成水準；截至 3Q26 Edge AI 佔比約 10%。

二、3Q26 毛利率低於預期，係
因出貨較多倉儲客戶產品 
 3Q26 營收 14.13 億元，截至 3Q26 產品組合：車檢 21%、倉儲物流 23%、醫
療保健 12%、其他 44%(其中超過 25%為國防軍工)；獲利方面，毛利率 36.36%，
低於預期，係因 3Q26 出貨較多低毛利率 Honeywell 倉儲物流產品，營益率
19.05%，單季稅後 EPS 2.63 元。

三、2027 年仍由國防軍工驅動主
要成長 
 3Q26 毛利率受 Honeywell 倉儲物流產品出貨較多影響，Honeywell 2026 全年
訂單金額預估 2~3 億元，2027 年 Honeywell 目標回到往年全年 6 億營收水準，
目前仍在與客戶洽談新訂單；4Q26 軍工航太及車用等出貨比重提升，預期毛利
率較 3Q26 回升；整體預期 2026 全年營收+30%YOY，其中出貨量成長 10%，
其餘 20%來自 ASP 提升(包含漲價與產品組合轉佳)。 
融程電目前 BB Ratio 1.1，訂單能見度約 6~8 個月，由於缺料狀況緩解，客戶
下單能見度恢復正常水準，預期 2027 年受惠軍工航太、無人載具及 Edge AI 需
求成長，產品組合持續優化將推升 ASP 及毛利率。產品組合方面，截至 3Q26
其他業務營收佔比 44%，2027 年佔比有望提升至 50%，主由國防軍工驅動，
2027 年國防軍工預期再成長 30~40%，甚至有望上看超過 50%成長，融程電具
全球領先海事船舶電腦技術，拓展至地面工作站及無人機整機，無人載具現有月

3597 映興(AVIN)

報告日期：2026/11/17 研 究 員：洪瑞陽 (Ryan Hung) 
壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 資料中心新專案自 3Q26 開始放量，3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ 
+28.8%、YoY +39.6%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元；10 月營收進一步
提高至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，本研究預估
4Q26 營收 3.97 億元，下半年營運動能明顯高於上半年。 
 2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估台達電集團訂單
營收占比由 2025 年的 41.5%提升至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年
約 65%，成為未來兩年主要營收增量來源。 
 受惠新訂單放量，本研究預 2026／2027 年營收分別為 11.8 億
元／17.8 億元，2027 年 YOY+50.8%，新增業務以台達電相關
OEM 線束為主，毛利率將維持一定區間較難提升，預估 2026
／2027 年毛利率 23.9%／23.4%，獲利成長主要來自營收放
量，稅後 EPS 分別為 1.61／3.31 元。

一、2H26 新專案開始放量，營收動能明顯轉強 
3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，毛利率 24.43%，
較 2Q26 維持前期水準；營業利益 2,993 萬元，營業利益率 9.17%，
較 2Q26 的 7.43%提高 1.74 個百分點，稅後淨利 2,156 萬元，EPS 
0.55 元。累計前三季營收 7.78 億元、EPS 0.90 元，營運較 2025
年同期明顯改善。

受惠資料中心新專案持續放量，7／8／9 月營收分別為 9,788 萬元
／1.05 億元／1.24 億元，呈現逐月成長。10 月營收進一步提升至
1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，創單月新高。本研究依
10 月實際營收、既有業務營運水準及新專案出貨進度推估，4Q26
營收約 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8% ，毛利率 23.65%，營業利益率
11.1%，稅後 EPS 約 0.71 元，下半年營運成長動能持續增強。

二、AI 電力需求成長，新專案放量，2027 年全年貢獻 
AI 算力需求提升帶動資料中心電力密度增加，電源架構朝高功
率、高電流及高可靠度方向發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及
PSU 等電源與備援系統需求同步增加。映興產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中
心機櫃、備援電力模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，
公司產品亦可延伸至大電流與高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

映興在 AI 資料中心的供應模式以電源系統相關線束及連接元件為

2 
主，並非直接供應 CSP，因此營運成長主要隨下游客戶資料中心
電源系統出貨放大。本研究預期 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，
2027 年除具全年化效果外，出貨仍持續 Ramp，因此預估新專案
相關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2025 年的約四成提高至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年約 65%，成為未
來兩年主要營收增量來源。

三、既有應用維持成長，較高毛利產品支撐產品組合 
除 AI 資料中心外，公司既有產品橫跨工業應用、移動載具、車載
電子及儲能等領域。移動載具涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩
托車、滑板車及船外機等所需的防水連接器與線束模組；車載電
子則供應車用風扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；工業應用包括氫燃料
電池、馬達、機械手臂與家電相關線束及代理元件。

公司自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具微型大電流、高插
拔次數、IP68 防水及抗火花等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主運算
工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、IATF 
16949 及 UL 等認證。

本研究預期車載及移動載具等較高毛利產品持續成長，可部分抵
銷 AI 資料中心新增 OEM 線束占比提高造成的毛利率負向影響。
3Q26 實際毛利率仍達 24.43%，顯示新增訂單放量尚未造成毛利率
明顯下降；惟隨 2027 年 OEM 營收占比進一步提高，產品組合仍
有毛利率些許負向影響。因此映興未來兩年營運型態將呈現「營
收快速成長、毛利率大致維持、費用率下降」，獲利成長主要來
自營收規模放大，而非毛利率擴張。

四、預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 1.61／3.31 元 
2026 年受惠資料中心新專案自 2H26 開始貢獻，本研究預估全年
營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5% ；毛利率 23.9%、營業費用約 1.82
億元，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%降至 15.5%，帶動營業利益約 9,837
萬元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%，EPS 1.61 元。

2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估全年營收 17.75 億
元，YoY +51.1%；毛利率 23.4%、營業費用約 2.20 億元，費用率
進一步降至 12.4%，營業利益約 1.95 億元、營業利益率 11.0%。
預估稅後淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.31 元，YoY 約+106%。 
五、投資建議：買進，目標價 59 元 
隨台達電訂單新專案貢獻，映興有新的營運動能，帶動營收與 EPS
正向成長，以 2027 年 EPS3.31 計算，根據歷史區間與同業評價，
給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 59 元

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、公司簡介 
映興電子成立於 1986 年，主要從事連接線束模組、代理元件及自
有防水連接器，營運據點涵蓋台灣、中國深圳與昆山、泰國及美
國。公司產品可分為三類：第一為 OEM／ODM／OBM 連接線束
模組；第二為代理元件，包括 Sensata 溫度保護元件、日本 Kurabe
特殊電線及 Anderson Power 大電流連接器；第三為自有防水連接
器。公司目前聚焦 AI 伺服器、工業應用、移動載具、車載電子及
綠能儲能五大應用市場。

公司核心能力在於複雜線束加工、防水連接器設計及代理元件整
合。自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具備 IP68 防水、抗
火花、高插拔次數及微型大電流等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主
運算工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、
IATF 16949 及 UL 等認證。相較一般單純線材加工廠，公司可利
用一線品牌代理權作為客戶導入入口，再延伸至線束加工與自有
連接器，提高單一客戶合作深度。

五大應用中，AI 伺服器主要供應資料中心 HVDC／PDU／CDU 電
源機櫃、UPS／BBU 備援電池櫃、TPS 基地台機房及 PSU 內部線
束；工業應用涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬達、機械手臂及家電所需線束
與代理元件；移動載具包括無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、
滑板車及船外機所需防水連接器與線束；車載電子則供應車用風
扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；綠能儲能則以太陽能儲能、大電流連
接器及電池櫃線束為主，相關技術亦可延伸至 AI 資料中心 BBU
備援電力應用。

資料來源：公司官網

二、AI 新專案放量，台達電集團訂單營收占比持續提升 
AI 資料中心為映興目前最重要的新增成長來源。隨 AI 算力需求
提升，資料中心電力系統逐步朝高功率、高電流與高可靠度方向
發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及 PSU 等電源與備援系統需求
同步增加。公司目前相關產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中心機櫃、備援電力
模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，因此可隨下游資
料中心電源系統需求擴張而受惠。公司亦已具備 UPS 電池系統、
IT Rack 、PDU 及電池模組串接等產品經驗，可延伸至 AI 資料中
心大電流及高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

公司並非直接供應 CSP，而是透過台達電集團訂單切入終端雲端
服務業者供應鏈。映興供應內容集中於資料中心電源系統內部線
束與連接元件，因此營運節奏與台達電集團資料中心電源業務高
度連動。除了資料中心電源機櫃外，公司在台達電集團既有業務
亦涵蓋 PSU、車載充電系統、基地台機房、儲能櫃及自動化設備
等產品，客戶合作範圍並非僅限於 AI 資料中心單一應用。

本研究估算，2025 年台達電集團訂單營收占比約四成；2026 年上
半年新專案尚未正式進入主要營收貢獻期，因此相關營收仍以
TPS、車用及既有電源產品為主。隨資料中心新專案於 3Q26 開始
放量，7、8、9 月公司合併營收逐月提升，3Q26 營收達 3.26 億元，
QoQ +28.8%。10 月營收進一步增加至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、
YoY +76.69%，本研究預期台達電集團訂單相關營收成長動能自下
半年起明顯轉強。

本研究對資料中心新專案採 Bottom-up 推估，將台達電集團訂單
營收區分為既有業務與資料中心新增業務，並依已公告營收、既
有業務正常營運水準及後續專案 Ramp 節奏進行推估，而非直接
以客戶占比反推公司營收。由於 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，

5 
2027 年同時享有全年化與出貨增加兩項效果，本研究預估相關新
專案營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2026 年約 50%進一步提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要
營收增量來源。

惟台達電集團訂單相關業務主要仍以 OEM 線束為主，毛利率相對
低於自有防水連接器、車載及部分移動載具產品，因此台達電集
團訂單營收占比快速提升將對產品組合形成一定毛利壓力。不
過，3Q26 毛利率仍達 24.43%，維持前期水準，加上集團既有產能
可因應新增訂單，營收規模放大有助於固定製造成本攤提。本研
究預估 2026／2027 年毛利率約 23.9%／23.4%，營業利益率則由
8.3%提高至 11.0%，顯示本輪成長主要來自 AI 帶動營收放量及費
用率下降，而非毛利率成長。 
三、車載與移動載具高毛利產品及其他業務正向發展 
移動載具產品涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、滑板車、
輪椅及船外機所需的防水連接器與線束模組。公司在此領域主要
競爭力來自自有防水連接器，可提供 IP68、防水、抗火花及高插
拔次數設計，適合戶外、震動及高濕度等環境，產品附加價值高
於一般 OEM 線束。公司原有兩輪載具為既有核心市場，近年亦逐
步向無人機與四足機器人等新應用延伸。

考量自行車庫存逐步正常化、無人機非紅供應鏈需求提高，以及
機器狗等新型移動載具應用擴張，本研究預估移動載具中短期營
收可維持中個位數成長，並以約 5～8%作為 2027 年相關業務成長
假設。由於此類產品較多採用公司自有防水連接器及客製化線
束，其毛利結構優於一般 OEM 產品，因此除營收成長外，亦可作
為未來產品組合的重要支撐。

車載電子方面，公司主要供應車用風扇、On-board Charger 及煞車
系統線束，已取得 IATF 16949 車用認證，可切入對品質、可靠度
及認證要求較高的汽車供應鏈。公司現有產品已涵蓋車載充電、
煞車系統及相關電力電子應用，隨全球汽車朝電動化與智慧化發
展，電力電子、充電系統及熱管理需求仍具中長期成長趨勢。

考量車用產品認證週期較長、導入後客戶黏著度相對較高，加上
電動車及高階車用電子滲透率持續提升，本研究預估車載電子中
長期營收成長約 10～15%。部分高階車用產品專案能見度亦延伸
至未來數年，預期車載業務可持續成為公司較穩定、且毛利率優
於一般 OEM 線束的產品線。

工業應用為公司既有營收的重要來源，產品涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬

達、機械手臂、燃油引擎所需線束模組與代理元件，以及空調壓
縮機、吹風機、吸塵器等家電使用的代理元件；客戶應用橫跨燃
料電池、工業自動化與消費型家電。

本研究預期工業應用仍受惠工業自動化、燃料電池及資料中心相
關電力需求，維持穩健成長。不過須注意公司應用別與客戶別分
類並非完全對應，部分 AI 資料中心及基礎建設相關產品亦可能歸
類於工業應用，因此本研究在財測時不直接套用單一工業應用成
長率，以避免與台達電集團訂單 AI 專案重複計算。

綠能儲能方面，公司產品包括太陽能儲能系統、大電流儲能連接
器及電池櫃串接線束，其技術與 AI 資料中心 BBU 在大電流傳輸、
電池模組串接及高可靠度連接等方面具有共通性。隨儲能與資料
中心備援電力需求增加，公司過去累積的電池櫃及大電流連接技
術可同時支援傳統儲能及 AI 資料中心相關應用，提供中長期新增
業務機會。

整體而言，本研究預期其他客戶業務將由車載、移動載具、工業
及儲能等既有應用共同支撐。2026 年在既有應用回升下，預估其
他客戶營收約 5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%；考量 2027 年基期提高，
且工業分類中部分 AI 營收已另於台達電集團訂單模型估算，本研
究採較保守成長假設，預估 2027 年其他客戶營收約 6.27 億元，
YoY 約+7%。此部分雖非未來兩年最大營收增量來源，但較高毛
利產品比重有助於抵銷 AI OEM 線束占比上升造成的產品組合壓
力。 
叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.61 元 
本研究將營收拆分為「台達電集團訂單」與「其他客戶」兩部分。
台達電集團訂單再區分既有業務及資料中心新專案；其他客戶則
依車載、移動載具、工業及儲能等既有應用的實際營運水準與成
長趨勢估算。台達電集團訂單營收占比為兩部分個別預估後除算
得出，而非預先假設占比後回推營收。

3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，較本研究原預
估 3.22 億元高約 1.5%；毛利率 24.43%，優於原預估 23.2%，惟營
業費用 4,976 萬元，亦高於原預估 4,200 萬元，營業利益 2,993 萬
元、營業利益率 9.17%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元。雖然營收與毛利率優
於原預估，但費用同步增加，顯示新增訂單放量後仍需考量營運
規模擴大所帶來的費用成長。

10 月營收 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，亦高於本研究

原估約 1.23 億元。考量資料中心新專案出貨進度優於原先預期，
本研究上修 4Q26 營收至 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8%。毛利率考量 3Q26
實際維持 24%以上，且產能利用率提高有助於固定製造成本攤
提，預估 4Q26 毛利率 24.0%；營業費用則依 3Q26 實際水準估約
5,000 萬元，營業利益約 4,534 萬元、營業利益率 11.4%，稅 後 EPS
約 0.73 元。下半年營收合計約 7.24 億元，較上半年增加約 60.3%。

本研究預估 2026 全年營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5%，其中台達電
集團訂單營收約 5.89 億元，占營收比重約 50%；其他客戶營收約
5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%，維持雙位數成長。

雖新增台達電集團訂單以 OEM 線束為主，產品組合對毛利率形成
一定的負向影響，但 3Q26 實際毛利率仍維持 24.43%，反映營收
規模增加可部分抵銷產品組合影響。本研究預估 2026 年毛利率約
23.9%，較 2025 年的 23.6%小幅提升。營業費用預估約 1.82 億元，
YoY 約+21.9%，增幅低於營收，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%下降至
15.5%，帶動營業利益約 1.00 億元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%。

業外損益預估約-598 萬元，考量前三季實際稅率及 4Q26 有效稅率
35%的假設，預估全年有效稅率約 31.7%，稅後淨利約 6,404 萬元，
EPS 1.63 元。獲利成長主要來自營收放量、固定成本攤提改善及
營業費用率下降，而非單純依賴毛利率提升。

二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.31 元  
2027 年營收成長主要來自資料中心新專案。由於 2026 年新專案僅
自下半年開始貢獻，2027 年除完整貢獻全年外，出貨仍持續 Ramp 
up。本研究考量 3Q26 實際營收與 10 月營收均優，預估新專案相
關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍；既有台達電集團訂單業務則維持穩
定，預估全年台達電集團訂單營收約 11.48 億元。

其他客戶方面，本研究預估 2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年考
量車載、移動載具及其他既有業務持續成長，但 2026 已有較高基
期，因此採較正常化成長假設，預估全年營收約 6.27 億元，YoY
約+7%。

兩者加總後，預估 2027 年營收 17.75 億元，YoY +51.1%，台達電
集團訂單營收占比提高至約 65%。相較原預估 16.94 億元，本次主
要反映資料中心新專案出貨進度提前，而非 ASP 上升。

季度營收預估呈逐季上升，1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 分別為 4.05
／4.29／4.58／4.83 億元。新專案出貨增加使台達電集團訂單營收
占比逐步提高，但考量 OEM 比重上升，毛利率維持；另一方面，

三、台達電集團訂單為主要成長，其他業務維持穩健  
台達電集團訂單方面，本研究預估既有業務維持正常營運水準，
資料中心新專案則自 2H26 進入放量階段。考量 3Q26 實際營收及
10 月營收均優，預估 2026／2027 年台達電集團訂單營收分別為
5.89／11.48 億元，YoY 約+65%／+95%，占合併營收比重由 2026
年的約 50%提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要營收增量來
源。

其他客戶方面，本研究依公司既有應用營運趨勢進行估算，預估
2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年增加至約 6.27 億元，YoY 約+7%。
2027 年增速較 2026 正常化，主要考量 2026 已有較高基期。

3Q26 OP 2.7조, 당사 추정치 35% 상회 
서프라이즈의 근거가 판매보증비 감소에서 발생. 3Q26 기말환율의 낙폭 (-12% qoq)으로 
인한 역설적 비용절감의 규모가 전년동기 대비 1.2조 원을 넘어서며 환율의 조사모삼 효과
로 만들어진 컨센서스 상회 실적. 
긍정적인 부분: 컨틴전시, 신차 효과, 인센티브, 생산 정상화 
지속적인 컨틴전시 체제를 유지하며 비용절감 실천 (=지속된 요소). 4분기 추가적으로 나아
질 요인은 1) 내수 신차효과로 판가 상승 및 유기적 수익성 개선, 2) 공장 가동률 정상화를 
통한 고정비 부담 절감, 3) 해외시장 신차효과를 통한 딜러 인센티브 절감. 
경계할 부분: 재료비, 환율, BEV 판매비중 확대 
재료비 측면에서 1) 2Q26 시작된 미국-이란 전쟁 관련 원재료비 상승은 4Q26F에도 하락
세를 보이지 못할 것이며, 2) 구리, 철강 등의 금속 원자재의 가격 상승 또한 4Q26F 지속될 
것. 또한 3Q26과 반대로 qoq 기말환율 대비 낙폭 확대할 평균환율 (mtd 1,338원) 효과로 
환 관련 감익 본격화, IONIQ 3의 출시는 인센티브 절감인 동시에 믹스 관점에서 부정적. 
투자의견 BUY 유지, 목표주가 440,000원으로 소폭 상향 
목표주가 상향 근거는 컨틴전시 체제 유지를 통한 FY27F 자동차사업 부문 이익 예상치 상
향. 부정적 외생변수 (환율, 재료비)가 지속된다는 전제 하에 FY27F OPM 하락을 예상하나, 
(FY26F 5.6%, FY27F 5.2%) 향후 신차효과와 생산 정상화에 따른 자동차 사업부문 수익성 
반등의 여력 존재. 주가 상승 트리거는 1) CES 전후 BD 관련 기대감, 2) 신차 출시 이후 견
조한 판매량, 3) 환율, 원재료비 등 외생변수의 개선.

### Page 1

(012450) 
한화에어로스페이스 
2026.10.30

AI 로보틱스/방산/조선/운송 양승윤 
6139 / syyang0901@eugenefn.com

여전히 비중확대 의견, 그리고 Top-Pick 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 1,660,000 원(유지) 
 현재주가 760,000 원(10/29)

 3Q26 연결 매출액은 6조 943억원(-6.0%yoy), 영업이익은 4,520억원(-
46.2%yoy, OPM 7.4%)을 기록. 영업이익 컨센 1조 1,614억원을 하회.  
 지상방산의 손익이 크게 악화. 영업이익은 217억원(OPM 1.7%) 기록. 주요 
납품이 4분기에 집중된 가운데 일회성 비용 약 500억원(대전사업장 복구·
정상화 약 300억원, 국내 사업 충당금 약 160억원)과 환율 영향이 겹침.   
 다만 내용을 살펴보면 수출 사업의 수익성 훼손은 크지 않았다는 판단임.  
 일회성 제외 영업이익은 약 717억원(OPM 5.8%)이며, 일회성 비용은 대전
사업장이 국내 물량을 전담한다는 점에서 전액 국내 귀속으로 추정. 내수 
OPM을 일회성 제외 -5% 내외로 가정하면, 내수 영업이익은 약 -826억원
(일회성 포함 OPM -12.7%), 수출은 1,043억원(OPM 17.6%)으로 추정됨. 
 1Q26 대비로는 국내 마진 하락이, 2Q26 대비로는 수출 물량 감소가 가장 
크게 작용(도표 참고). 국내는 저마진 사업 매출이 늘고 고마진 반복 양산 물
량이 줄며 일시적으로 믹스가 악화. 수출은 폴란드 등 주요 인도가 정체되어 
매출이 감소한 가운데 저수익 사업 중심으로 믹스가 악화된 영향. 
 4Q26부터 회복 구간 진입 전망. 폴란드 K9 2차 실행계약 물량의 현지 인
도가 본격화되며, 회사는 2026년 폴란드 K9 30문 이상, 천무 발사대 60대 
이상(*연초 40대에서 상향) 인도를 계획. 수출입 데이터상으로도 폴란드·루
마니아향 출하가 대규모로 진행 중이며 폴란드향 부속류 수출도 정상적으로 
이어지는 것으로 확인. 일회성 소멸과 고마진 수출 집중으로 수출 OPM은 
30% 내외로 회복할 것. 다만 국내는 정상 가동(2027년 상반기 목표) 전까
지 한 자릿수 초반 마진에 머물 것으로 전망. 
 중장기 수익성 우려도 크지 않다는 판단. 2027~2029년 매출 연 15% 성
장 가이던스는 유지됐고, 내년 폴란드 K9·천무 인도 물량은 모두 올해 대비 
증가할 계획. 폴란드 사업 이후 높아진 수출 마진 레벨은 후속 사업에서도 
유지될 전망. 수주는 중동 지정학 불안으로 지연되고 있으나 대공체계 등 문
의가 이어지고 있어, 2027년부터 중동이 수주의 중심이 될 것으로 기대. 
 동사에 대한 투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 166만원을 유지함. 3Q26 지상방산 
쇼크는 국내 적자와 수출 물량 공백이 겹친 일시적 결과로, 수출 경쟁력 훼
손으로 보기 어려움. 실적 충격에 따른 주가 조정은 분할 매수 기회로 판단.  
 한화에어로스페이스의 주가는 4월 7일 사상 최고가 153.7만원에서 10월 
29일 76.0만원으로 -51% 조정 받은 상황. 12MF P/E 기준 14.3x, 27E 
P/E는 13.9x로 밸류에이션 관점에서도 매력적인 구간 진입. 그동안 3분기 
실적 우려 등으로 부진했다면, 이제는 실적 바텀아웃과 대형 수주 랠리 속 
다시 한 번 반등을 기록할 차례. 동사를 방산업종 Top-pick으로 유지함.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

### Page 1

AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.
Business creations by sector: a boom that is building momentum and spreading

We have calculated the year-on-year change in the number of new business start-ups. The table categorises these results as positive (red), neutral (white) or negative (blue). The darker
the colour, the larger the change. The data series are defined as follows: Germany = new entries in the companies register (start-ups) since 2008; France = business start-ups, including

micro-entrepreneurs, since 2000; United Kingdom = businesses registered for VAT/PAYE since 2017; United States = business applications (tax identification number) since 2004. Sectors
have been grouped together to ensure that the classification is standardised across all countries. For the third quarter, the German data only include the months of July and August.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com
Article written with the help of Ece Usta (intern).

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years.1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.

MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.

Document 411: Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Tracking Europe’s AI transition_ A new STOXX 600 earnings-call framework (2026-11-11).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 16
Status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank 
Research Institute

Tracking Europe’s AI transition:  
A new STOXX 600 earnings-call 
framework

1 
Authors 
Michael Kirker 
Economist

November 11, 2026 
How quickly is AI moving from experimentation into everyday business, and 
where are companies reporting tangible gains? The answers matter for 
institutional investors and policymakers assessing productivity, operating 
efficiency and labour demand. Yet comparable evidence across Europe remains 
limited, making it difficult to track the pace and scale of the transition. 1 
We address this gap with a proprietary framework that turns what is said on 
STOXX 600 earnings call into three structured indicators: AI adoption 
intensity, reported productivity benefits, and the impact on workforces. 
Large language models analyse what management teams say about AI’s 
operational role, applying a common scoring framework across companies, 
sectors and countries. Our new approach provides a timely pan-European 
comparable tracker that captures not just whether AI is being mentioned, but its 
real-world impact as the adoption of the new technology unfolds. 
Key Takeaways: 
— Adoption is broadening, with signs of faster progress. AI-use scores rose 
gradually from a low base before strengthening over the past 12 months. The 
increase extends across sectors, although the pace and level of adoption 
remain uneven. 
— Workforce impacts remain limited in corporate disclosures. The 
framework does not show a broad negative workforce signal attributable to 
AI. The aggregate score remains close to the level of no impact on the labour 
force across all of our sample. However, this does not rule out AI displacing 
workers at a future date. 
— Geographic differences persist within the listed-company sample. Since 
2022, German companies in the STOXX 600 have generally recorded above-
average AI-use and reported productivity scores. Dutch and Spanish 
companies in the STOXX 600 have tended to score lower on AI use, although 
Spain has recently gone through a rapid catch up. 
— The technology J-Curve: The findings are consistent with an early stage of 
the AI transition. Companies report increasing adoption of the new 
technology, but transformational productivity gains and extensive workforce 
restructuring remain uncommon.

1 1 Our US team regularly publish on data from the USCB showing the impact of 
AI on businesses, and our German team have recently begun their own 
chartbook. In the UK, the Office for National Statistics publishes on the topic too.

### Page 2

Deutsche Bank AG Page 2

Methodology 
Sample period: Our sample begins in January 2022, providing a baseline before 
ChatGPT’s launch on 30 November 2022. AI in this framework covers both 
established and emerging technologies, so the launch of ChatGPT is a reference 
point rather than the start of all corporate AI adoption. 
Timing of observations: STOXX 600 companies report on different financial 
calendars and at quarterly or semi-annual intervals. Their earnings calls also mix 
discussion of completed activity with forward-looking guidance. 
We assign each transcript to the calendar quarter in which the earnings call takes 
place, regardless of the financial reporting period discussed. The resulting 
indicators therefore track when information about AI use and its operational 
impacts becomes public rather than when implementation or the reported effect 
occurred. This provides a common dating convention across the sample while 
retaining differences in reporting frequency and disclosure timing.

Figure 1: Coverage of the STOXX 600 earnings calls

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: Share of the STOXX 600 is based on the current company weight in the index. Data collection for the current quarter (Q4 2026) is incomplete 
as some companies are still to report

Constructing the STOXX 600 AI corpus: We use a specialised AI transcription 
engine to process earnings calls from STOXX 600 companies, restricting the 
analysis to calls for which we obtain complete transcripts of sufficient accuracy. 
In Q3 2026, the latest full quarter, the dataset covered more than 500 companies, 
representing approximately 90% of the index’s current market capitalisation 
weights (Figure 1). In the current quarter (Q4) earnings calls are still taking place. 
Nearly 250 companies with an equivalent weight to around c.60% of the STOXX 
index having already reported. 
Historical coverage typically ranges from 300 to 500 companies and represents 
more than 80% of the index’s current market-capitalisation weights. Coverage 
peaks twice a year because some European companies report semi-annually.  
11 November 2026

### Page 4

Deutsche Bank AG Page 4

For examples of the AI’s scoring and justifications, see the tables presented in the 
Annex at the end of this report. 
Summary statistics 
Figure 2 shows the distribution of scores across all earnings-call transcripts in the 
historical sample, without weighting for company size or reporting frequency. 
These are transcript-level distributions rather than shares of companies, and they 
combine early and recent observations. They provide context for the quarterly 
indicators: 
• AI use score: Approximately 60% of earnings call transcripts do not 
discuss AI use. Among the minority that do discuss it, most fall into the 
“Active implementation” range (a score of 4-6 out of 10). 
• AI’s productivity impact: Nearly 70% of earnings calls do not mention a 
tangible AI-related productivity benefit. Among calls that do, scores are 
generally in the medium-impact band, indicating notable operational 
improvements. Transformational gains, scored at 8 or above out of 10, 
are rarely reported. 
• AI’s labour impact: Only around 10% of earnings calls discuss AI-related 
workforce adjustments. Reported changes typically receive scores of 1–
2 in magnitude on the −5 to +5 scale, with a slight tilt towards hiring and 
upskilling rather than redundancies.

Figure 2: Raw distribution of scores across the entire sample

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Note: Results are based on the raw number of transcripts, and are independent of firm size, and biased towards firms reporting more often.

Taken together, the distributions are consistent with an early adoption phase: 
companies discuss AI implementation more often than transformational 
productivity gains or extensive workforce restructuring. A technology J-curve 
offers one interpretation of this pattern, with investment and organisational 
adaptation preceding broader benefits.  
This J-curve pattern is strongly corroborated by external micro-data, such as the 
Banco de España’s business activity survey (EBAE) of Spanish firms, which 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 5

Deutsche Bank AG Page 5

similarly indicates that AI has a more immediate impact on upfront investment 
and productivity than on headcount. 
AI use: A broad-based rise, with signs of faster recent adoption 
The new AI-use indicator points to a broadening transition across STOXX 600 
companies, with early signs that adoption has accelerated recently. Scores rose 
only gradually from 2022 and 2024, but the latest observations suggest a 
stronger pace across industries and country groups.  
The rise is broad-based rather than confined to one sector. Technology has led 
much of the sample, while financials show tentative recent outperformance and 
utilities have caught up from a low starting point. Among the large country groups, 
German companies have persistently recorded above-average scores.  
AI use across European industries 
To trace sectoral dispersion, we group companies by STOXX industry 
classification and calculate a weighted average of their scores for each calendar 
quarter, using their latest STOXX 600 index weights. 2  
We focus on four sectors spanning different business models: financials, the 
largest services sector; industrials, the largest physical-assets sector; technology, 
a likely early adopter; and utilities, an infrastructure-heavy sector where adoption 
may proceed more slowly.  
Figure 3 presents the AI use score for these four key industries alongside the 
range for all industry scores (represented by the shaded area). 
• The AI use score is generally low over time but is gradually rising from 
around 1 point in 2022 to around 3 in 2026. 
• AI-use scores are rising across industries, but differences between 
sectors are widening. The common direction therefore points to 
broadening adoption rather than uniform progress: sectors can 
participate in the transition while remaining at different levels of use. 
In terms of the industries of interest: 
• Technology’s early lead has narrowed. The sector’s AI-use score was 
near the top of the industry range through to 2024 but has recently 
plateaued as the broader STOXX 600 average caught up during 2025–
26.3 
• Financials and industrials broadly track the index average. Both sectors 
have remained close to the aggregate AI-use score over history, with 
financials generally a little below average, and industrials a little above. 
But financials show tentative signs of moving above average in recent 
quarters.

2 The industries are: (1) Basic Materials, (2), Consumer Discretionary, (3), Consumer Staples, (4) Energy, (5) 
Financials, (6) Health Care, (7) Industrials, (9) Real Estate, (10) Technology, (11) Telecommunications, (12) 
Utilities. 
3 Q4 2025 saw a large increase in the AI use score for the “Technology” industry, jumping up to around 6. This 
one-off spike was driven by ASML’s announcement of a significant strategic partnership with and investment 
in Mistral AI” to 'embed AI across our entire holistic portfolio in order to increase the performance and 
productivity of our systems and the yield of our customers' processes'. Because of ASML’s large market cap 
(around 49% of our Technology category), it distorts the weighted average significantly.

Deutsche Bank AG Page 6

• Utilities have caught up from a low base. After recording lower AI-use 
scores than other sectors for much of the sample, utilities converged 
towards the STOXX 600 average during 2026. The result shows that the 
rise in reported adoption extends beyond the sectors most closely 
associated with AI.

Figure 3: AI use score (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The value for each industry is computed as a weighted average of all firms within the industry where the weights are derived from the current 
STOXX 600 share of each company. The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a cross-check, our findings align with the European Commission’s October 
Eurobarometer Flash survey of MSEs and Mid-Caps and the Eurostat annual ICT 
surveys, which show low overall intensity but a similar expansion in service- and 
tech-related sectors.  
AI use across countries 
Companies in the STOXX 600 tend to be large multi-nationals. That makes it 
difficult to pin down where physically the AI transformation is taking place within 
their organisation. Instead, our country comparison is done using two 
classifications of a company’s country: 
• Country of domicile: AI is a transformation technology, and this type of 
change is likely to be driven in organisation from the top down. We 
therefore consider a company’s country as that in which it is domiciled 
(which is likely to be where the headquarters is also). 
• Country of primary listing: While companies can generally list in other 
countries, there tends to be a home bias in their choice of exchange.  
Figure 4 presents the AI use intensity metric by both of these measures of country 
(the company’s domicile and primary exchange listing). Again, the shaded area 
shows the range across all classifications, with the largest countries/exchanges 
singled out. 
German-domiciled companies and the Xetra-listed (Frankfurt stock exchange) 
group record above-average AI-use scores in most quarters since 2022.  
Spain’s AI-use score has risen noticeably over recent quarters and now exceeds 
the aggregate average, reversing its earlier below-average position.  
11 November 2026
Focus Europe

### Page 7

Deutsche Bank AG Page 7

AI-use scores rise across country groups, with indications of faster growth from 
2025. The shared direction suggests a broad European listed-company trend, 
even though differences in score levels and timing persist between countries.

Figure 4: AI use intensity (0-10) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

As a geographical cross-check, our index matches the broad trends of the 
Eurostat ICT annual survey, which places Germany as a clear frontrunner among 
its large peers. But some differences do exist. For instance, Eurostat shows the 
Netherlands with a very high AI adoption. As Figure 5 demonstrates, there is a 
positive correlation between the AI use score metric derived here, and that from 
Eurostat, even though the Eurostat data measures if firms are adopting AI, and 
our new use measure also incorporates the concept of how intensively firms are 
using AI.

AI productivity impact: Reported benefits are rising, but 
transformational gains remain uncommon 
Higher intensity of use does not necessarily mean firms are benefiting more from 
AI. But our AI productivity impact metric does show a similar pattern to our AI 
usage metric, although it tends to be slightly lower, indicating that companies 
may not yet be reaping the full rewards for their AI investment. 
Financials stand out more clearly on reported productivity benefits than on AI-use 
intensity, while German companies again record relatively strong scores, but only 
in more recent history.

Figure 6: AI's impact on productivity (0-10) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter

AI productivity impact across European industries 
Reported AI-productivity benefits have become more prominent across 
industries. Figure 6 shows scores rising gradually during 2022–25, followed by 
11 November 2026

Deutsche Bank AG Page 9

indications of a faster increase from late 2025. The profile resembles the AI-use 
trend in Figure 4, suggesting that the stronger adoption narrative is accompanied 
by more discussion of operational gains. 
The aggregate trajectories of the AI use and AI productivity impact scores are 
similar, but sector rankings differ. Technology’s early relative strength in AI use is 
not matched by its productivity score, while financials report above-average 
productivity benefits despite more modest use scores. 
AI productivity impact across countries 
Figure 7 plots the self-report impact of AI on company productivity by country. 
Between 2022-23, German companies recorded average productivity impact, 
even though their AI use score was relatively high. However more recently, their 
AI impact score has persistently been above average. The UK grouping shows 
persistent early outperformance in AI impact during 2022–23, followed by 
convergence towards the aggregate average.

AI labour force impact: On net, AI may be benefiting 
employment, for now… 
Across industries and countries, the recorded score for AI’s impact on the labour 
force remains relatively small. On average, the reported impact on companies’ 
employment has been fairly neutral, with a marginal negative bias over the last 
year or so.

Figure 9: AI's impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by industry

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

Deutsche Bank AG Page 11

AI labour force impact across European industries 
Despite rising productivity, AI has not triggered the negative employment shock 
feared by the public. Aggregate labour impact scores across nearly all sectors 
hover near zero, with a slightly positive balance over history, and a slightly 
negative balance more recently in 2025-26 (Figure 9). But overall the magnitude 
of AI’s impact on labour remains low. For the STOXX 600 aggregate average, the 
score’s maximum deviation from 0 (no impact) is 0.43 on our 0-5 scale. 
AI labour force impact across countries 
Workforce-impact scores are also small across the main country groupings, with 
most countries slowing a slight negative tilt in recent years (Figure 10).

Figure 10: AI impact on the labour force (-5 to +5) by country

Source: Deutsche Bank Research. Notes: The markers denote the readings for the current incomplete quarter. Negative (positive) scores are associated with job losses (gains) 
associated with a company's AI use.

The lack of significant impact so far from AI on companies’ labour forces so far is 
consistent with results from our previous work looking at the other side of the 
labour market. Data from our dbDataInsights household survey showed that 
workers across Europe reported a fairly low level of fear about losing their job to 
AI over the next year. The analysis in that report found results that were more 
consistent with workers adapting to the new AI world by upskilling and 
11 November 2026 
Focus Europe

### Page 12

Deutsche Bank AG Page 12

demanding higher wages. Those results would not be consistent with a world in 
which AI was actively replacing labour. 
These findings of a low impact on labour are highly consistent with recent 
academic research (e.g., Yotzov et al., 2026), which found that nearly half of 
surveyed UK and German firms reported absolutely no impact on headcount from 
AI over the past three years, 
Conclusion 
The newly developed framework provides a recurring way to monitor company 
disclosures about AI’s impact on productivity and labour. The central finding of 
this report is a broadening AI transition rather than clear evidence of an 
economy-wide productivity breakthrough. The new metrics indicate that AI use 
remains limited but is rising across the European countries and industries 
represented in the sample. Companies report low but growing productivity gains 
and little aggregate workforce impact so far. This pattern is consistent with a 
technology J-curve, with firms still integrating the new technology, rather than 
proof that this trajectory will unfold. Limited reported workforce effects today do 
not rule out subsequent layoffs or wider organisational restructuring. 
Quarterly updates to this framework will track Europe’s AI transition and provide 
timely signals of whether companies are reporting more substantial productivity 
and employment effects. 
Beyond AI, the framework could be adapted to extract signals about employment 
decisions, investment decisions and the general economic outlook from earnings 
calls. These potential extensions could form the basis of alternative nowcasting 
models for important macroeconomic data; they are applications that will be 
explored in future work.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY
It has been an action-packed few weeks for markets. The US
2026 mid-term elections saw a repudiation of President Trump
with the Democrats now set to take control over both houses
of Congress. The outcome and easing Middle East tensions

saw Brent crude oil fall from just under US$120/bbl to roughly
US$95/bbl, back around the post-Iran-conflict average. The
associated easing in inflation fears has supported a continued
descent in bond yields and rally in risk sentiment – albeit with
significant ‘wrinkles’ around a high US inflation read and an
AI-led pull-back in equity markets that has been compounded
by the sudden resignation of NVIDIA’s CEO.
Many central banks have already begun re-tightening policy

– with the Bank of Canada and Bank of England the latest
additions to the pack – but what comes next will differ widely
across jurisdictions. In the US, the combination of strong
activity and sticky inflation have led FOMC members to more
strongly advocate for further tightening. We have added a
January hike to the December move already in our forecasts,
before a long pause and eventual cuts in 2028.
Europe shares has similar inflation concerns but not so much

strength on the growth front. The ECB sees activity near
trend and is uncomfortable with inflation at 3.8%yr, so we
expect a December hike as insurance. Unlike market pricing
though, we think this will be the last, with energy and
investment-related price pressures easing through 2027 and
scope to ease back towards neutral in 2028. China faces a
very different challenge – not too much demand but too little.
Industrial strength and technology investment are providing

support but we expect policy direction and stimulus will be
needed to turn household demand, with this expected to
come in 2027 and property the most likely catalyst.
The RBA meanwhile followed its September hike with another
in November, but the Governor’s call to ‘take stock’ signals a
December pause and we think the hiking phase is now more
clearly over. We continue to pencil in a move to cuts in the
second half of 2027. While a February hike is not completely

out of the question, policy is clearly restrictive and downside
risks are crystallising. Reflecting this, we have downgraded
our GDP, labour market and housing market forecasts for 2027.
Australia: The housing downturn has been sharper than
expected and is set to spill over more materially to the broader
economy. The scale of these spillovers should become clearer
over coming months, keeping the RBA on hold as downside
risks to growth build. Business investment remains a source

of resilience, led by data centres and renewables, but rising
costs and growing community opposition have seen some
projects shelved. Still, the supply-side picture is constructive,
with strong investment in the market sector driving capital
deepening and productivity gains, albeit uneven ones.
Commodities: The commodities complex was a story of two
halves in October, with sharp gains across energy offsetting
weakness elsewhere and lifting our broadest commodities

index 0.6%mth. Reflecting continued energy-market tightness,
we expect Brent to average US$101/bbl in Q4 and have raised
our Q4 Japanese LNG forecast to US$27.
Global FX Markets: Of late, risks have favoured the US
dollar and FOMC member concerns have been decidedly one-
sided towards higher inflation. The DXY has consequently
continued to test the upper limit of its 18-month range, but
remains inside it.
New Zealand: A busy month has included a tight – and

currently inconclusive – general election, a surprise hold by the
RBNZ in October and a somewhat disappointing Q3 labour
market report. However, the medium-term outlook remains little
changed, with a recovering economy and above-target inflation
likely to lead to further monetary tightening in coming months.
Pacific: Distance from major markets, small domestic
economies and a heavy reliance on imported fuel and food
mean global disruptions tend to reach the Pacific at a higher

cost. However, most Pacific countries face the latest shock
with somewhat better buffers.
United States: Data suggests the US carried strong
momentum in to H2 2026. But questions are being asked over
the profitability of AI deployment, household real incomes are
trending flat to down, and term interest rates continue to weigh.
China: China GDP surprised to the upside in Q3, keeping
authorities on track for 2026, but more importantly offering a
little more time to accelerate domestic demand in to 2027.

Asia: We expect the BoJ to raise its policy rate by 25bps to
1.50% in December, before gradually taking it to 2.0% through
2027. The case for further tightening rests on the evidence that
firms are more willing to raise prices, labour scarcity supports
persistent wage growth, which should in turn support spending.
Europe & UK: The ECB left its key policy rates unchanged in
October. Another hike is forecast in December. Against market
pricing, we expect this will prove the last for the current cycle.

Taking stock

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
All major central banks are now in a rate-hiking phase. The
stance of policy nonetheless varies considerably across
economies, as does the policy outlook.
The Bank of England and Bank of Canada increased their
policy rates for the first time this cycle and the RBA a fifth time

since the last Market Outlook. The pace of increase is generally
moderate: the FOMC and RBNZ paused at their latest
meetings – the RBNZ unexpectedly so – but both are expected
to lift policy rates further. Policy is likely still accommodative
in New Zealand and Canada, consistent with the economic and
labour market slack evident in these economies. In Australia it
is clearly restrictive, as discussed below.
The US is contending with sticky inflation, as evidenced by

recent CPI and labour market data. We have long been of the
view that the US is likely to be structurally more inflationary
than many peers, and concerns about inflation are increasingly
coming to the fore in FOMC members’ comments. Accordingly
we have added another hike in January to our US rates view, on
top of the one in December already forecast (see page 20).
The main, but not sole, driver of the pivot to tightening has been
the energy price shock. That said, exclusion-based core inflation

measures (excluding food and energy) are also above central
bank targets. Market pricing of future central bank moves had
been sensitive to oil prices and the outlook in the Middle East
earlier in the year. That correlation broke down during the bond
sell-off, which was mostly a phenomenon of real yields, but
seems to have  re-emerged in the pricing for policy in 2027.
Spot and daated Brent oil prices have fallen markedly in recent
days as tensions have eased and negotiations appear to have

restarted. This has not affected very near-term pricing of central
bank decisions, and an initial moderation of mid-2027 pricing
retraced after the strong US CPI print and some Fed speeches.
Further along the yield curve, last month’s sell-off in bond
markets has largely reversed. Unlike the increase in yields,
when longer-run market-based measures of inflation
expectations relatively stable, the more recent rally has
involved some decline in these expectations measures. Most of

the adjustment has been front-loaded: the 1-year expectations
derived from swaps have declined 45bps for Australia and
nearly 60bps for the US since the beginning of November.
However, inflation expectations derived for future years have
also moderated. The 5-year-5-year forward measures derived
from swaps and breakevens have both declined, with the latter
noticeably below the 2.5% RBA target midpoint. The same
measures for the US have also declined over this period, but by

less, and remain further above the Fed’s 2% target, consistent
with our view about inflation pressures there.
Global asset market rotation ...
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FINANCIAL MARKETS
... while the RBA nears a pause
The rally in long bond markets has occurred at the same time as
equity markets have tended to sell off, particularly in the tech
sector. Another wave of scepticism around the sustainability
of the data centre build-out has formed and found expression

in the equity prices of many semiconductor manufacturers and
other related firms. One possible trigger for the latest sell-off
may have been the release of Anthropic’s prospectus ahead
of its IPO. As well as the more existential risks flagged in the
documents, its stated plans for further data centre investment
have concentrated focus on the scale of the data centre and AI
boom more broadly, and the enormous funding task involved.
The RBA Monetary Policy Board (MPB) hiked at its November

meeting following up the late September hike as expected. As
we noted at the time, the MPB was responding to the apparent
crystallisation of some of the risks that it had flagged back in
August. In particular, the re-emergence of pressures on fuel
prices led to fears of a further wave of pass-through of energy
costs into the prices of other goods and services. The Q3
outcome for underlying inflation outstripped expectations and
Q4 is also likely to be uncomfortably high. With inflation already

materially above target, the RBA is worried that inflation
expectations could be rising, pointing to liaison and business
survey evidence. So far, though, other business surveys suggest
that pass-through has been less severe than the original wave
of price increases at the outbreak of the Middle East conflict.
In the post-meeting media conference, the Governor flagged
that it was a good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening
to date. Both we and the market read this as a signal that the

MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote
at the meeting also suggests that some external members are
more attentive to downside risks than the internal members.
We think the most likely outcome from here is that the RBA
has ended its rate-hiking phase, and that a cutting phase will
begin in the second half of 2027. There is still a risk of a hike
in February, and we believe the MPB will at least discuss the
option at that meeting. The staff and senior officials remain

sensitive to inflation risks, and have a downbeat view of
supply capacity growth. The latter meant that the RBA had to
upgrade its forecasts for the labour force participation rate in
its However, policy is clearly restrictive, and while there are
upside risks to inflation, there are clearly downside risks, too.
With markets now expecting that the RBA is likely at the end
of its hiking phase, while most peers are expected to have 2–3
hikes to go, short-end yield differentials have narrowed. This

has been reflected in the AUD exchange rate, which has tended
to underperform in recent days. We have tempered our near-
term outlook for the AUD, but note that the TWI remains abourt
4% above the 2022–2024 average, and that this appreciation
will be contributing to the disinflationary impact of tight policy.
